- **verbose**: A VPC is a private IP space you own inside a region. Everything else — routing, isolation, egress control — is built from how you subdivide it. Two rules pay off for years: choose a CIDR big enough that you never renumber, and never let two networks you might one day connect share an address range. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/cloud-networking-design/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Disaster recovery has exactly two quantitative inputs, and every architectural choice downstream is a consequence of them. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/disaster-recovery-planning/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Almost every object-storage mistake comes from carrying filesystem instincts into a system that only superficially resembles one. Object storage (S3, GCS, Azure Blob) is a flat map from a string key to an immutable blob of bytes, reached over HTTP. That is the whole model. What looks like a directory tree is an illusion the tooling paints over a flat namespace. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/object-storage-patterns/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Start from the question, in writing, before choosing anything. Most wasted research is a well-run study that structurally could not answer what was asked. - **verbose**: The single highest-leverage convention in code review is prefixing every comment with its severity. Without it, the author must reverse-engineer your intent, and the safe default is to treat everything as blocking — which is how a two-nit review turns into another round trip. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/code-review-practices/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Threat modeling asks what could go wrong in a design. Vulnerability assessment asks what *is* wrong in the thing you actually built and deployed. The first is predictive and cheap; the second is empirical and finds the gap between the design you modeled and the system you shipped — the misconfigured bucket, the forgotten staging host, the library that went stale. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/vulnerability-assessment/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: An API has no UI to hide things behind. Every identifier a client has ever seen is an identifier it can replay against every endpoint. The dominant API vulnerability class — broken object-level authorization — is not a missing authentication check; the attacker is fully authenticated as themselves. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/api-security/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Expected error decomposes into bias, variance, and irreducible noise. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/ensemble-methods/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: JavaScript executes on a single thread with one call stack. A function pushed onto the stack runs to completion before the engine does anything else — no timer, promise, or event handler can interrupt synchronous code partway. Concurrency comes from *scheduling* callbacks to run later, never from preemption. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/javascript-event-loop/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: Rust splits failure into two categories. `Result&lt;T, E&gt;` represents conditions a caller can reasonably anticipate and recover from — missing files, malformed input, a rejected request. `panic!` represents a bug: a violated invariant that means the program is in a state its author never intended. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/rust-error-handling-patterns/verbose) · ★2)
- **verbose**: A goroutine is a lightweight thread the runtime multiplexes onto OS threads. Channels are the typed conduits goroutines use to pass values and, crucially, ownership of data. The guiding maxim is "don't communicate by sharing memory; share memory by communicating." ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/go-concurrency-patterns/verbose) · ★2)

- **verbose**: A git SHA pins the transformation. It says nothing about what went into it. - **verbose**: Threads do not automatically see each other's writes. The compiler, JIT, and CPU may reorder instructions and cache values in registers, so a write in one thread can be invisible to another indefinitely. The Java Memory Model defines *happens-before*: if action A happens-before action B, then A's effects are visible to B. You get those edges only through synchronization. ([snoodleboot-io/prompticorn](https://github.com/snoodleboot-io/prompticorn/tree/HEAD/prompticorn/skills/java-concurrency-and-memory/verbose) · ★2)
